CHAPTER 7. No.4.
SOLICITORS.
ARTICLED CLERKS AND SOLICITORS.
RULES MADE UNDER THE SOLICITORS ORDINANCE.
-1. (1) These rules may be cited as the Solicitors Rules.
(2) In these rules " the Ordinance" means the Solicitors Ordinance.
PRELIMINARY EXAMINATION.
'2. (1) Subject to the provisions of the Ordinance, all persons seeking to become Articled Clerks shall pass an examination in General Knowledge before being bound under Articles of Clerkship.
(2) Applications for exemptions under paragraph (f) of section 31 (1) of the Ordinance shall be made by petition verified by statutory declaration, copies of which shall be lodged with the Council of the Society seven days before the application is sent in.
3. The subjects of the preliminary examination are;-
A.-Compulsory Subjects
Arithmetic: Up to School Certificate standard, including Simple and
Compound Interest, Bankers' Discount and the Metric System, but not recurring decimals. Algebraical symbols and methods may be employed, Logarithms.
English Grammar and Composition. English Literature: e.g. Shakespeare. English History: e.g. Outlines of the History of England from 1714 to 1900. Latin: A paper will be set containing questions on Grammar and Parsing;
an easy unprepared passage for translation into English; simple sentences, and an easy continuous passage for translation into Latin; and passages from a selected book, e.g. :-Caesar de Bello Gallico, Book III.
B.~Optional Subjects
(One optional Subject and no more must be taken)
French: A paper will be set containing questions on Grammar, unprepared-
passages for translation into English, easy English sentences for translation into French and .either continuous passages for translation into French, or one or more subjects on one of which a short composition in French is to be written.
Algebra: Up to and including quadratic equations. Geometry: (1) Practical construction of angles, triangles,circ1es and
quadrilaterals from sufficient data .
. (2) Theoretical: Theorems and easy riders on :-Angles ata
point; parallel straight lines; triangles and rectilinear figures; loci; areas; and the circle; similar triangles.
Spanish: As for French. or such subjects as may from time to time be prescribed by the Judges of the Supreme Court and by the Principals of the Queen's Royal College and St. Mary's College, and shall be published in the Royal Gazette in the first week of the month of January in every year. Cand_dates are to notify the Clerk to the Judges at least two v,eeks before the date of the examination which optional subject they intend to take.
4. (1) The preliminary examination shall be held in Poit-of-Spain in the months of May and November, on such day as the Registrar by direction of the Chief Justice may fix.
(2) Every candidate shall give notice in writing to the Registrar of his intention to offer himself for examination at least two months before the date of the examina-tion, and the Registrar shall transmit such notice forthwith to the Secretary of the Incorporated Law Society of Trinidad (in these rules referred to as "the Society").
(3) Upon the receipt of such 1'lotice as aforesaid the Secretary of the Society shall convene a meeting of the Council of the Society and shall require every candidate to attend such meeting.
(4) The Council shall examine every such candidate and otherwise enquire into his character and into his fitness to become a Solicitor, and if they are satisfied that he is a fit and proper person to become a Solicitor they shall so certify. Such certificate shall be in the prescribed form and shall be sealed with the Seal of the Society, be signed by the President or Chairman of any such meeting, and counter-signed by the Secretary. Upon the filing of such certificate in the office of the Registrar, the candidate shall be entitled to be examined.
(5) The preliminary examination shall be conducted by or under the direction of the said Principals, who shall be paid such fees as the Chief Justice shall fix.
(6) If the examiners are satisfied as to the proficiency and fitness of a candidate they shall certify the same to the Chief Justice and a certificate of having passed shall be issued in the prescribed Form: Provided that the examiners shall not issue such certificate unless a candidate has obtained not less than 60 per cent. of the marks in each of the papers set in compulsory subjects and not less than 45 per cent. in the paper set in an optional subject: Provided also that a candidate who fails to obtain the said certificate shall be deemed to have failed and shall not be allowed to offer himself again for examination otherwise than in the whole of the subjects set in the examination. .
(7) The examiners shall have power to eject any candidate from the examination room for misconduct. Any candidate so ejected shall not be entitled to pass at that examination or enter his name for another examination without first obtaining the permission of a Judge. Application for such permission must be made in writing to. the Registrar. . .
(8) A candidate who has failed on three occasions to pass the examination cannot again enter his name as a candidate for examination.
(8) The examiners certificate shall be initialled by the Registrar and handed to the successful candidate.
(10) The Supreme Court or a Judge thereof may on complaint made as to the conduct or character of the candidate, direct the Registrar to refuse to initial the certificate pending enquiry into the matter of the complaint by the said Court or a Judge thereof.
(11) Every person desirous of being articled by virtue of any qualifying examination certificate shall before his name is entered in the Articled Clerk's Book produce to the Registrar a certificate in accordance with sub-rule (4) of this rule.
(12) The fee payable to the Registrar on giving notice for a preliminary examination is $24.00 with an additional fee of $12.00 when such notice is given after the prescribed date. Should a candidate not present himself at the examination for which he has given notice, or should he fail to pass such examination, he shall be entitled, on giving at least two months' written further notice to the Registrar to present himself at a subsequent examination, and the renewal fee payable by him to the Registrar on giving such further notice for any subsequent examination is $24.00, provided that where such failure to present himself at the Examination is due to his illness, no renewal fee shall be payable.
(13) The cost of the examination shall be defrayed out of the examination fees. Should the cost of examinations in any year be less than the amount of fees deposited, the balance of such amount shall be applied to the purchasing of books for the Supreme Court Law Library.
(14) In addition to the fees payable to the Registrar there shall be paid to the Treasurer of the Society for the use of the Society the following fees, that is to say:-
By every person applying to be examined at a preliminary examina-
tion $10.00 every such fee shall be payable by the candidate on giving not~ce of his desire to be examined at the examination in respect of which the fee is payable. An additional fee of $5.00 shall be payable to the Society if notice is given after the prescribed date. If a candidate fails to pass such examination or fails to present himself at such examination (except due to illness) he shall pay to the Society on every sub-sequent application to sit such examination a fee of $10.00.
ADMISSION TO ARTICLES OF CLERKSHIP.
5. Every person desiring to be admitted to articles of clerkship shall, before becoming bound thereby, obtain the consent of the Society thereto.
6. (1) Not less than three weeks before any person becomes bound by articles of -clerkship he shall give notice in writing under his hand to the Society in the prescribed form of his intention so to do.
(2) Such notice shall be sent to the Secretary of the Society accompanied by the applicant's birth certificate and by the names and addresses of two persons -each of whom will vouch as to his good character and as to his fitness and suitability for service under articles.
(3) Such certificates of good character shall be separate certificates and the two persons referred to in sub-rule (2) hereof shall be responsible persons resident in the Colony who are not near relations of the applicant but who have known him personally for not less than two years and have had opportunities of judging of his character, fitness and suitability. One of these certificates should, where practicable, be supplied from a responsible official of the School, College or University at which the applicant was educated and the other from a Clergyman, Justice of the Peace, Solicitor or other person of known standing. .
(4) The Society may, if they think fit, before issuing their consent, require the applicant to attend before them in person and to furnish such additional evidence 'of character, fitness and suitability as they may consider necessary.
(5) If the Society are satisfied with the evidence so furnisheci they will issue to the applicant in writing their consent to his entry into articles in the prescribed form,
7. (1) The period of 4t years specified in paragraph 4 (a) of the 2nd Schedule -to the Ordinance shall apply in the case' of persons who:-
(a) Prior to the 1st day of January, 1939, have passed any of the following
examinations :-
The School Certificate Examination of the University of Durham (with
Honours) ;
The School Certificate Examination of the University of Cambridge Local
Examination Syndicate;
The School Certificate Examination of the University of Oxford Local
Examination Syndicate;
The Higher School Certificate Examination of the University of London; The Higher Certificate Examination of the Universities ,of Oxford and
Cambridge Schools Examination Board, Certificate A;
The Higher School Certificate Examination of the Oxford Delegacy of
Local Examination, Certificate A; , '" '
The Higher School Certificate Examination of the Cambridge Local
Examinations Syndicate Certificate A;
The Higher School Certificate Examination of the University of Bristol,
Certificate A;
The Higher Certificate Examination of the University of Durham~
Certificate A;
The Higher School Certificate Examination of the Joint Matriculation
Board of the Universities of Manchester, Liverpool, Leeds, Sheffield and Birmingham, Certificate A;
The Higher ~ertificate of the Central Welsh Board, Certificate A;
Provided that in the case of the School Certificate Examination of the-
University of Cambridge Local Examination Syndicate and the. School Certificate Examination of the University of Oxford Local Examination Syndicate the person shall have passed with credits (at least) in English Language, Latin and three other subjects selected from the following groups. of subjects namely:-
(1) English Subjects;
(2) Languages other than English;
(3) Science and Mathematics.
(b) Prior to the 1st day of January, 1941, .. have passed any of the following:
examinations :-
The Matriculation Examination of the University of London (in' the-
first division and no alternative paper to be taken) :
The School Certificate Examination of the Cambridge Local Examination.
Syndicate;
The School Certificate Examination of the Oxford Local Examinations
Syndicate;
Provided that in the case of the School Certificate Examination of the·
Cambridge Local Examination Syndicate and the School Certificate Examina-tion of the Oxford Local Examinations Syndicate the persons shall have passed with credits (at least) in English Language, Latin and three other subject~ selected from the following groups of subjects namely:-
(1) English Subjects;
(2) Languages other than English;
(3) Science and Mathematics.
(c) Subsequent to the 31st day of December, 1938, have passed any of the-
following examinations:-
The Higher School Examination of the University of London; The Higher Certificate Examination of the Oxford and Cambridge Schools.
Examination Board;
The Higher School Certificate Examination of the Oxford Delegacy' of
Local Examinations; . _
The Higher School Certificate Examination of the Cambridge Local
Examinations Syndicate; .
The Higher School Certificate Examination of the University of Bristol;
The Higher Certificate Examination of the University of Durham;
The Higher School Certificate Examination of the Joint Matriculation.
Board bf the Universities of Manchester, Liverpool, Leeds, Sheffield and. Birmingham; .
The Higher Certificate Examination of the Central Welsh Board; pro-
vided that no alteration has been made in the character, standai:d or regulations of or relating to such Examination between the date of these rules and the date when the person passed such Examination . . (d) Have passed Law Moderations at Oxford University, the qualifying: Examination in Law at Cambridge University, the Intermediate or equivalent Examination for the degree of LL.B. at any University in England and Wales.
(2) The period of four years specified i~ paragraph 4 (6) of the Second Schedule to the Ordinance shall apply in the case of persons who have passed one or more of the examinations specified in (a), (b) and (c) of sub-rule (1) of. this rule and in addi'tion one or more of the examinations specified in paragraph (d) of the same sub-rule~
(3) In the case of every examination specified in sub-rule (1) of this rule :--
(a) The person shall have passed the examination prior to entering into
his articles of clerkship, and (b) The certificate shall indicate that all the subjects were taken at one examination; and, where Latin is not a compulsory subject, that the person satisfied the examiner in that subject.
INTERMEDIATE EXAMIKATION.
8. Every articled clerk may present himself for the Intermediate examination as follows:-
(a) If articled for five years or four and a half years, after one year's service.
(b) If articled for four years or three years, at any time after the registration
of his articles of clerkship.
9. The Intermediate examination shall be held in the months of May and November of each year in the Jury Room, Red House Buildings, or at such other times or in such other place as the Registrar by the direction of the Chief Justice may appoint.
10. The Intermediate examination shall be on the followingsubjects:-
(a) The elements of the Law of Real Property;
(b) The principles of the Law of Contract; and the elements of the Law of
Torts;
(c) Public Law, comprising:-
(i) The Law and Custom of the Constitution; and
(ii) The organisation of the Courts of Justice vl'ith the outlines of their
past history,' the organisation of the Legal Profession and the elements of procedure; and
(iii) Trust Accounts and Book-keeping; or such other subjects as may from time to time be prescribed.
11. There will be no set books for the legal portion of the examination, but the Registrar shall, on application being made to him in that behalf supply to any candidate a list of the text books recommended for study in any particular year.
12. (1) The Intermediate examination shall consist of the following portions:-
(a) Legal portion: If the candidate fails to pass this examination he may
attend a subsequent examination; but if he shall not have passed the examina-tipn before the expiration of twelve months next after the date when one half of his term of service shall have expired, his Final examination shall be postponed for so long a period as may intervene between the expiration of such last mentioned twelve calendar months and his passing the examination, or for such shorter time as the Court or a Judge shall direct.
(b) Trust Accounts and Book-keeping portion: A candidate failing to pass
this examination may give notice for any subsequent examination but must have passed it ,before giving notice for the Final examination.
(2) Candidates may present themselves for both portions of the Intermediate examination at the same time, or for each portion separately.
13. Every candidate shall at least three months before the date of the Inter-mediate examination at which he proposes to be examined give to the Registrar written notice of his desire to be examined, and shall leave with the Registrar the articles and any assignment thereof or supplemental articles duly stamped and registered under which the candidate has served or is serving his clerkship or any portion thereof, together with a certificate of having passed the qualifying or Preliminary examination, and answers to the questions in that behalf specified in the prescribed forms signed by the Candidate and the Solicitor or Solicitors with whom he has served or is serving his clerkship or any portion thereof.
14. The Registrar shall refer such answers to the Council of the Society. and if the Council after such further enquiries as they shall see fit, are -of opinion that any such answers are unsatisfactory, they shall certify the same to the Chief Justice, and the candidate shall not be permitted to present himself for the examination, provided that the Chief Justice may in any case on special grounds allow the candidate to be examined notw-ithstanding any such answers.
15. The fee payable to the Registrar on giving notice for the Intermediate examination is $60.00 for each portion of the examination with an additional fee of $12.00 when such notice is given after the prescribed date. If a candidate does present himself at the examination for which he has given notice, or if he fails to pass such examination he shall be entitled, on giving at least three months' written notice to the Registrar and otherwise complying with rule 13 to present himself at a subsequent examination, and the fee payable by him to the Registrar on giving such notice for any subsequent examination shall be $60.00 for each portion of the Intermediate examination. Should the candidate fail to present himself at any examination due to his illness no fee shall be payable on renewal of his application.
16. In addition to the fees payable to the Registrar there shall be paid to the Treasurer of the Society for the use of the Society the following fees, that is to say:-- .
By every person applying to be examined at an Intermediate examination,
$24.00.
Every such fee shall be payable by the candidate on giving notice of his
desire to be examined at the examination in respeCt of which the fee is payable and also on every subsequent examination except where the candidate failed to present himself at any examination owing to his illness when no fee is payable on renewal.
FINAL EXAMINATIO~.
17. The Final examination shall be held in May and November of each year in the Jury Room, Red House Buildings, or at s:uch other time or in such other place as the Registrar by the direction of the Chief Justice may appoint.
18. Notwithstanding anything contained in the Ordinance or in these rules, any articled clerk otherwise complying with these rules may with the approval of the Society and subject to such terms and conditions as the Society may impose, take his Final examination in London on the same dates as the examination is held in Port-of-Spain: Provided that any additional expense incurred in connection therewith shall be borne by such candidate.
19. The Final examination shall be on the follmving subjects:--
(A) Compulsory Subjects (Five Papers).
(1) The Law of Real and Personal Property, with special reference to the
principles and practice of conveyancing; and the Principles and Rules of Equity;
(2) (a) The Law relating to Negligence and Nuisance; including liability
for dangerous property and dangerous things.
(b) The Law relating to Defamation. (er The Law of Evidence.
(e) Criminal Law.
(e) Proceedings in criminal cases before Magistrates other than Magistrates
sitting at Quarter sessions. .
(3) (a) The Law of Wills and of Intestate Succession and of the Administra-
tion of Assets; including the procedure and practice of the Probate Court in non-contentious matters.
(b) The Law relating to Death Duties.
(c) The Law relating to Income Tax.
(4) (a) The Law relating to Contracts generally; and
(b) The Law relating to:--
(i) Sale of Goods;
(ii)N egotiable Instruments ;,.
(iii) Master and Servant, including VVorkmen's Compensation;
(iv) Hire Purchase;
(v) Insurance;
(yi) Insurance (other than JI,larine Insurance).
(5) The Law relating to:-
(a) Companies;
(b) Agency and Partnership;
(c) Bankruptcy;
(d) Arbitration.
(B) Optional Subjects (one only to be taken).
(6) Either;-
(a) (i) Conflict of Laws; and
(ii) The Law and Procedure in Matrimonial Causes usually determined
or administered in the Probate, Divorce and Admiralty Division of the High Court of Justice, and before Magistrates; or
(b) Local Government Law and Practice; or
(c) The Law and Practice of Magistrates' Courts, including indictable and
summary offences, matrimonial jurisdiction, bastardy, juvenile courts, treatment of offenders, civil jurisdiction, collecting officers' _ duties, the issue of pro,~ess, evidence in criminal cases, and licensing.
20. There will be no set books for the Final examination, but the Registrar shall on application being made to him on that behalf supply to any candidate a list of the text books recommended for study in any particular year.
21. Every candidate shall at least three months before the date of the Final examination at :which he proposes to be examined give to the Registrar written notice of his desire to be examined, stating his place of residence, together with the name or names, and place or places of residence, of the person or persons with whom he has served during the continuance of his articles; and shall also leave with the Registrar the articles and any assignment thereof or supplemental articles duly stamped and registered under which the candidate has served or is serving his clerkship or any portion thereof, together with certiiicates of his having passed the qualifying or Preliminary examination unless exempted under the provisions of the Ordinance, and the answers to the questions in that behalf specified in the prescribed forms, signed by the Candidate and the Solicitor or Solicitors with whom he has served or is serving 'his clerkship or any portion thereof. On giving such notice the candidate shall notify the Registrar of the optional subject which he intends to take.
22. The Registrar shall refer the answers mentioned in rule 21 to the Council of the Society and if the Council after such further enquiries as they shall see fit are of opinion that any such answers are unsatisfactory, they shall certify the same to the Chief Justice, and the candidate shall not be permitted, to present himself for examination; provided that the Chief Justice may in any case on special grounds allow the candidate to be eX8,mined notwithstanding such answers.
23. The fee payable on giving notice for the final examination shall be $120.00 with an additional fee of $12.00 when such notice is given after the prescribed date. If a candidate does not present himself at the examination for which he has given notice, or if he fails to pass such examination he shall be entitled on giving at le2cst three months' 'wTitten notice to the Eegistrar and (unless his failure to present himself for such examination was due to his illness) on payment of a further fee of $120.00 to present himself at any subsequent examination. If he has been exempted from the Intermediate examination an additional fee of $20.000 shall be payable to the Registrar on his first giving notice for the Final examination.
24. In addition to the fees payable to the Registrar under the last preceding rule there shall be paid to the Treasurer of the Society for the use of the Society the sum of $24.00 by every person applying to be examined at a Final examination but no renewal fee is payable if such persoll failed to attend such examination due to illness. There shall also be payable tf) the Society the sum of $5.00 by every candidate who was exempted from the Intermediate examination, on first giving notice of intention to sit the final examination.
25. (1) Every.person presenting himself for the Intermediate or Final examination shall observe the written instructions issued and communicated to him by the Registrar and approved by the Chief Justice.
(2) The examiners shall have the power to eject any candidate from the examination room for breach of such instructions or other misconduct. Any candidate so ejected shall not be entitled to pass at that examination, or to enter his name for another examination without first obtaining the permission of a Judge. Application for such permission shall be made in \VTiting to the Registrar.
ADMISSION TO PRACTICE.
26. (1) Notice of intention to apply for admission to practice as a Solicitor shall be given to the Registrar in the prescribed form at least three clear weeks before the first day of the month in which he wishes such application to be considered by the Full Court; and such notice may be given before the applicant has passed the Final examination or the term of service under articles has actually expired, and may be lodged either personally or by post. ~o other documents are to be lodged with such notice.
(2) If the applicant withdraws or does not proceed with such application, the notice thereof shall be deemed to be cancelled, and, where he seeks admission at a later date, fresh notice shall be given in conformity with this rule.
27. The Registrar shall on receiving the notice specified in ~ule 26 (1) affix a copy thereof in some conspicuous place in the Law Library or in such other place or places as shall from time to time be directed by the Chief Justice.
28. Any person objecting to the admission of an applicant as a Solicitor shall give notice of his objection to the Registrar in writing under his hand accompanied by an affidavit of the matters of fact relied on in support of such objection. Copies of such notice and of the affidavit shall at the same time be sent by the objector to the applicant.
29. (1) Every such objection shall be placed before the Court and the Court shall thereupon appoint a day for hearing the objection, and the Registrar shall forth'with notify the same to the applicant and to the objector.
(2) The applicant, the objector and the Registrar may appear in person or be represented by Counsel at the hearing, and the Court shall make such order thereon as it shall think fit.
30. (1) Every application for admission shall be by petition supported by affidavit in the prescribed forms.
(2) Every such petition shall be accompanied by the certificates and declarations specified in sections 4 (1) and 6 of the Ordinance and s4all be filed at the Supreme Court Registry at least one clear week before the first day of the month specified in the notice of intention to apply for admission.
(3) Application to the Society for the certificate of fitness mentioned in section 4 (1) of the Ordinance shall be made in the prescribed form.
DISCIPLINARY COMMITTEE.
31. (1) Any three members of the Disciplinary Committee appointed under section 10 of the Ordinance, shall form a quorum, and such members shall elect their own Chairman ..
(2) Every application to the Committee under section 11 of the Ordinance, shall contain the matters of fact relating to the charge against the solicitor and shall be signed by the applicant and verified by his affidavit in the prescribed forms .
(3) If the applicant is unable to sign his name, or if the application has been prepared, whether wholly or in part, by a person other than the applicant, the person who so prepared the application shall attest the affixing of the applicant's mark or his signature to the application and shall certify on the back thereof that the 'Contents of the application were read over and explained by him to the applicant who appeared to understand and approved of the same. Such person shall subscribe his name and address to the application and shall also state the fee or reward received or agreed upon for his said services. If in the opinion of the Committee an application has in jact been wholly or partly prepared by a person other than the applicant, but the name and address of such other person have not been subscribed thereon as aforesaid, the Committee may reject such application.
(4) The application and affidavit and also a correct and legible copy of the application shall be sent in the first instance to the Registrar.
(5) The Registrar shall lay such application and affidavit before the Committee .and the Committee shall, if they think that a prima facie case is.shown in C • eur of the application require such Solicitor to answer the allegations therein. The Registrar .shall thereupon cause the copy of the application to be served on the Solicitor by notice in the prescribed form.
(6) The Solicitor shall send to the Registrar his answer to the allegations in the application together with a copy thereof within seven days after receipt by him of such application. In the event of his failing or neglecting so to do the Committee .may refer the application to the Full Court or deal with the same as it shall think fit.
(7) The Committee on receiving the answer of the Solicitor may refer the same to the applicant for reply thereto.
(8) On receiving the answer from the Solicitor the Committee shall, if they .are of the opinion that a formal enquiry into the complaint is necessary, :fix a day for hearing and shall request the Registrar to give notice thereof to the complainant .and to the Solicitor. . . (9) The Registrar shall thereupon cause notice to be served upon the parties in the prescribed forms, which notice shall be served upon them not less than twenty~ ·one days from the date fixed for hearing.
(10) Every party to the enquiry shall not less than fourteen days from the date fixed for hearing furnish to each other and to the Registrar for the use of the Committee copies of all documents on which they respectively propose to rely.
(11) Every party to the enquiry may inspect the documents included in the list furnished by any other, arid a copy of any such document shall, on application and on payment by the party requiring it of the proper charge, be furnished to the :,party by the other within three days after the receipt of such application.
(12) The Registrar shall at the request of the Committee require the attendance before him at the enquiry of any person who prepared the application in whole ·or in part, and shall on the application of either party issue subpoenas in the prescribed forms to such witnesses as the Committee may consider shou).d be heard.
(13) Every such enquiry shall be heard in camera and witnesses may be sworn, or their affirmations taken, by the Chairman of the Committee; and witnesses .(including the parties) may be subject to examination and cross-examination as nearly as may be if they were witnesses in an ordinary action.
(14) The Comn;tittee may proceed in their enquiries in the absence of either -party, if, having regard to all the circumstances of the case, they are of opinion -that such absence is the result of gross negligence cir of an intention to avoid or ·delay proceedings.
(15) The Committee shall state their findings in writing and a copy of the evidence (if any) taken by them and the report of the Committee signed by the ·Chairman shall be filed in the office of the Registrar together with all documents 'Put in at the enquiry.
(16) Upon any enquiry under these rules, the Committee may make such -order as to the costs of the enquiry as they may think fit.
(17) No application shall be withdrawn after it has been sent to the Registrar, except by leave of the Committee: Application for leave to withdraw shall be made on the day fixed for the enquiry, unless the Committee otherwise direct. The Committee may grant leave subject to such terms as to costs or otherwise as they :shall think fit ..
(18) The Committee may of their O\vn motion, or upon the application ot either party, adjourn the enquiry upon such terms as to costs, or otherwise, as to the C?mmittee appear just.
(19) If upon the hearing it shall appear to the Committee' that the allegations. in the application require to be amended, or added to, the Committee may pennit such amendment, or addition, and may require the sam.e to be embodied in a further application, if in the judgment of the Committee such amendment, or addition, is not within the scope of the original application, provided always that if such amend-ment, or addition, shall be such as to take the Solicitor by surprise, or prejudice the conduct of the case, the ComIJ?ittee shall grant an adjournment of the enquiry, upon such terms as to costs, or otherwise, as to the Committee shall appear just .. . (20) Upon the hearing or determination. of any application the Committee-may, without finding any misconduct proved against the Solicitor, neverthess order him to pay the cost of the proceedings, if, having regard to his conduct and to all the circumstances of the case, it shall seem just to the Committee so to do.
(21) If the Solicitor sllall fail to pay the costs ordered to be paid by the Committee within the time specified by any such order made under any of these rules, or where no time is fixed, within a reasonable time, the Registrar shall on the direction of the Committee cause the Solicitor to be summoned to appear before the' Full Court to be dealt with as the Full Court may think just. Notice of any such application shall be served on the Society, who shall be. entitled to appear by Counsel.
(22) In the event of the Committee ordering costs to be paid by an applicant where the Committee find that no prima facie case of misconduct has been made· out against the Solicitor and the applicant shall fail to pay the costs so ordered to be paid within the time fixed by such order, or where no time is so fixed, within a reasonable time, the Solicitor may apply, on notice to the applicant, to a Judge in Chambers for an order to enforce the payment of such costs and the Judge shall make such order as he may think just. . .
(23) Shorthand notes of proceedings may be taken by a person appointed by the Committee; and any party who appeared at the proceedings shall be entitled to inspect the transcript thereof. The shorthand writer shall, if required, supply to the Committee and to any party who appeared at proceedings, and to the Society but to no other person, a copy of the transcript of such notes on payment of such charges as the Committee may order. If no shorthand notes be taken, the Chairman. shall take a note of the proceedings, and the provision of this rule as to inspection and taking of copies shall apply to such note accordingly.
(24) The Committee may dispense with any requirements of these ruies. respecting notices, affidavits, documents, service, or time, in any case where it appears to the Committee to be just so to do. .
(25) The Committee may extend the time for doing anything under these rules.
(26) If the Committee are of opinion that a prima facie case has been made out, the Registrar shall cause the Solicitor to be summoned to appear before the Full Court, to be dealt with as the Full Court shall think just.
(27) The evidence takeu, and all documents put in at the enquiry may be used on the hearing of the application by the Full Court and the parties shall be at liberty to produce such further evidence as they may be advised.
(28) The Registrar shall forthwith give notice to the Society and the applicant of the day fixed for hearing of the application by the Full Court. .
(29) The Society shall be entitled. to appear by Counsel on the hearing of the application.
(30) If the order of the Court made on the hearing of the application be adverse to the Solicitor, the Registrar shall make such entry (if any) or alteration on the Roll of Solicitors as shall be required by the order.
(31) No fees shall be taken by the Registrar for any papers filed in the matter of the application.
32. (1) Any party to the enquiry ·who is aggrieved by any order of the Com-mittee may appeal to the Full Court by Notice of Motion which shall state the grounds of appeal and whether the appeal is from all or part only of the order. Every appeal shall be by way of re-hearing.
(2) The appellant shall enter the appeal within eight days from the day when the order appealed from was pronounced, by lodging a copy of the Notice of Motion at the Registry of the Supreme Court, and shall serve a copy thereof upon every party to the proceedings before the Committee, and upon the Secretary of the Society. There shall be at least fourteen clear days between the service of any such notice and the day named therein for the hearing.
33. Upon the entering of notice of appeal, the Registrar shall cause to be prepared and supplied for the use of the Judges hearing the appeal copies of the order appealed from, the findings of the Committee, any affidavit lodged with the Committee by any party and the notes of evidence taken on the hearing.
34. The Court may direct the Committee to furnish a report in writing giving their opinion upon the case generally or upon any point arising thereon. Such report shall be lodged at the Registry, and a copy thereof shall be sent to each party to the appeal.
35. Any person who desires to be heard in opposition to the motion and appears to the Court to be a proper person to be heard, shall be heard notwithstanding that he has not been served with the Notice of Motion and such order as to costs may be made as the Court shall think fit.
36. (1) The Court shall have power to extend the time for appealing and to grant leave to amend the grounds of appeal and to make any other order relating to the appeal on such terms as the Court shall think just.
(2) The Court may, if it thinks it necessary or expedient in the interest of justice, order any person who would have been a compellable witness at the hearing before the Committee to attend and be examined before the Court, or order the evidence of any such witness to be taken before an examiner or by affidavit whether such witness was or was not called at such hearing.
(3) The Court may allow or dismiss the appeal or may vary the order in such rnanner as the Court shall think just.
37. An appellant at any time after he has entered the appeal may abandon the same by lodging a copy of the notice of abandonment at the Registry, Port-of- Spain, and by giving notice of abandonment to all persons who have been served with the Notice of Motion. The appeal shall thereupon be treated as having been dismissed with costs.
38. (1) Every application to the Court under the Ordinance shall be made by petition under the hand of the applicant, accompanied by a statutory declaration verifying the facts relied upon in such petition. The applicant shall on sending the petition file the original with the Registrar and send to the Secretary of the Society copies of the petition and statutory declaration. The applicant, objector, secretary of the Society and the Registrar may appear in person or be represented at the hearing by Counsel.
(2) The Society shall have at least fourteen days' notice in writing of every application, motion, petition, summons or legal proceedings taken or made under the Ordinance, or any rules made thereunder and shall be entitled as of right to be heard upon such application, motion, petition, summons or legal proceedings.
39. The forms set out in the Schedule to these rules shall be the prescribed forms appropriate to the sUbjectcmatter specified therein.

3. Have you at any time since the date of your articles been absent without the permission of the Solicitor or Solicitors to whom you were articled or assigned? And, if so, state the length and the occasions of such absence.
4. Have you since the date of your articles been engaged or concerned in any profession, business or employment other than your professional employment as clerk to the Solicitor or Solicitors to whom you were articled or assigned? And, if so, give particulars thereof.

FORM 5.

served by Candidates for the Intermediate Examination.
1. During what period has served under his (or her) articles at the office where you carry on your business? If he (or she) has not served for any portion of the period which has lapsed between the date of his (or her) articles and the present time, state the reason.
2. Has the said at any time during his (or her) service under articles with you, been absent with or without your permission? And if so, state the length .and occasions of such absence.
3. Has the said during his (or her) service under articles with you, been ·engaged or concerned in any profession, business or employment other than his (or her) proftssional employment as your articled clerk? And, if so, give particulars thereof.
4. Has the said since the date of his (or her) clerkship, with the exception .above-mentioned, been faithfully and diligently employed in your professional business of a Solicitor ?
5. Has he (or she) hitherto, as regards character, shown himself (or herself) to be a fit and proper person to be admitted, at the end of his (or her) service, a Soli~itor of the Supreme Court ?
Dated the day of , 19
Signature Office Address
FORM 6.
ParticularS" to be furnished by Candidates for Final Examination.
1. What is now your age? 2. Have you served the whole term of your articles at the office where the Solicitor or Solicitors to whom you were articled or assigned carried on his, her or their business and, if not, state the reason.
3. Have you at any time during the term of your articles been absent with or without the permission of the Solicitor or Solicitors to whom you were articled or .assigned? And, if so, state the length and occasions of such absence.
4. Have you at any time during the term of your articles been engaged or concerned in any profession, business or employment other than your professional employment .as Clerk to the Solicitor or Solicitors to whom you were articled or assigned? And, if so, give particulars thereof.
5. Have you, since the expiration of your articles, been engaged or concerned, and for how long a time, in any and what profession, or trade, business or employment other than the profession of a Solicitor ?
Dated the day of , 19
Signature Present Address
16 (2)
FORM 7.
Particulars to be furnished by the Solicitor under WhOlll Articles were
served by Candidates for the Final Examination.
1. During what period has served under his (or her) articles at the office where you carryon your business? If he (or she) has not served for any portion of the term of his (or her) clerkship, state the reason.
2. Has the said at any time during his (or her) service under articles with you, been absent with or without your permission? And, if so,' state the length and occasions of such absence.
3. Has the said at any time during his (or her) service under articles with you, been engaged or concerned in any profession, business or employment other than his (or her) professional employment as your Articled Clerk? And, if so, give particulars thereof. .
4. Has the said during the whole term of his (or her) clerkship with the exceptions above-mentioned, been faithfully ana diligently employed in your professional business of a Solicitor?
5. Has the said since the expiration of his (or her) articles, been engaged or concerned, and for how long a time, in any and what profession, trade, business or employment other than the profession of a Solicitor?
6. Is the said as regards character a fit and proper person to be admitted a Solicitor of the Supreme Court?
Dated the' day of , 19
Signature Office Address

(a) Full name of applicant; if the applicant has since the registration of his articles of clerkship changed his name by Deed Poll duly enrolled which has been registered with the Society he must in the notice show the name in which he was originally articled as well as the name he has assumed, e.g., "I, A.B., originally articled as A.Z." or " I, A.B.C., originally articled as A.C."
(b) Address of applicant.

FORM 9.
Petition for Admission.
This is the Petition marked" A "
referred to in the prefixed Affida-, vit of (Name of Articled Clerk) sworn to the day of , 19
Commissioner oj Affidavits. TRINIDAD.
IN THE SUPREME COURT OF TRINIDAD AND TOBAGO.
No. of 19
In the Matter of the Solicitors Ordinance
and
In the Matter of the Petition of,
[Name oj Articled Clerk.]
To: His Honour the Chief Justice and Their Honours the Puisne Judges of the Supreme Court of Trinidad and Tobago.
THE HUlI1BLE PETITION of [Name oj Articled Clerk] of [his address] in the Island of Trinidad, Articled Clerk.
RESPECTFULLY SHOWETH as follows:- 1. Your :Petitioner has passed the Filial Examination of the Law Society, as appears by their Certificate dated the day of ,19, a true copy 6f which is hereunto annexed and marked" B."
2. Your Petitioner has, in pursuance of the rules made under the Solicitors Ordinance, obtained a Certificate of Fitness from the Council of the Society dated the day of " 19 ,and such Certificate is hereunto annexed and marked "C."
3. Your Petitioner is desirous of being admitted to practise and enrolled as a Solicitor of this Honourable Court.
WHEREFORE Your Petitioner humbly prays
that he be admitted to practise in the Courts of this Colony AND YOUR Petitioner as in duty bound will ever pray.
Dated this day of 19 This Petition is presented by [Name oj Articled Clerk] of [his address] in the Island of Trinidad, Articled Clerk.
NOTE.-It is not intended to serve anybody with this Petition.
FORM 10.

TRINIDAD.
IN THE SUPREME COURT OF TRINIDAD AND TOBAGO.
No. of 19
In the Matter of the Solicitors Ordinance
and
In the Matter of the Petition of
[Name oj Articled Clerk.]
I [Name oj Articled Clerk] of [his address] in the Island of Trinidad, Articled Clerk, make oath and say as follows:-
1. I 'am the Petitioner mentioned in the Petition hereunto annexed and marked It A."
2. The facts set out in the said Petition are true and correct. SWORN to at [place sworn] 1. the day of ,19 J
Before me,
Commissioner oj Affidavits.
